11. Migration & Integration Framework

Production orchestration engines (e.g., LangGraph, CrewAI, AutoGen, MCP instances) are deployed entirely inside the boundary limits of the Mutation Space. External tool registries are ingested by the compiler and transformed into native Manifest parameters, while session checkpointers are mapped directly to isolated Mode A/B memory states. Complex hierarchical topologies (such as supervisor–worker design patterns) are authorized for implementation if and only if they strictly adhere to the following architecture cluster: strict structural subset manifest nesting, independent atomic child micro-budgets, controlled output de-classification tracking via the SANITIZED_PROVENANCE marker, and cascading recursive Purge propagation links.

Migration Sequence for Existing Orchestration Deployments: Teams migrating active LangGraph, CrewAI, or AutoGen deployments to GVAA compliance must follow this sequence. First, instrument

PDF PAGE 78

the existing orchestration layer with a read-only Invariant Core Proxy in observation mode, logging tool invocations and shared state transitions without enforcing quota limits. Second, derive the initial Policy Manifest from the observed tool registry and state transition log; this manifest must be signed with the Ed25519 root key and registered against the designated High-Watermark baseline. Third, re-deploy the orchestration layer inside the Mutation Space boundary with quota enforcement active at Minimal Profile (ApproxIsolation) to validate operational continuity. Finally, promote to Standard Profile only after all Dual-Axis Quota parameters have been validated against observed invocation rates and the Residual Risk Registry has been populated with accepted operational exposures approved by the CISO. Partial or incremental enforcement — wherein some tool paths are proxied and others are not — is an architectural violation and must not be used as a transitional state.

12. Positioning within Industry Practices

GVAA does not replace existing agentic safety frameworks or alignment patterns. It operates as an independent, deterministic containment substrate beneath the application layer, composable with — but structurally separate from — the frameworks described below.

Framework / StandardPrimary Layer of OperationRelationship to GVAA
NIST AI Risk Management Framework (AI RMF 1.0)Organisational governance and risk categorisationGVAA's Residual Risk Registry and Maturity Model (M1–M4) map directly onto AI RMF's Govern and Measure functions. GVAA provides the technical enforcement substrate that AI RMF governance policies reference but do not specify.
OWASP Top 10 for LLM ApplicationsApplication-layer vulnerability taxonomyGVAA's Executive Summary maps mitigations to LLM01, LLM02, LLM07, and LLM09. GVAA addresses these at the runtime substrate level; application-layer defences remain the responsibility of the consuming engineering team.
RLHF / Constitutional AI / GuardrailsModel weight alignment and output filteringThese mechanisms operate within the Mutation Space as defined by GVAA's Four-Domain Topology. GVAA makes no assumptions about their correctness and provides containment guarantees independent of their output.
Microsoft Azure AI Content Safety / Google Cloud Model ArmorManaged API-layer content filteringThese services function as external validation components deployable within or adjacent to GVAA's Validation Space. They do not satisfy GVAA's Invariant Core requirements and cannot substitute for a compliant containment substrate.

Teams adopting GVAA should maintain all existing application-layer safety controls. GVAA's architectural claim is additive: it bounds the specified classes of infrastructure-level effects available to a compromised or manipulated agent, subject to the documented assumptions and verification status, independent of whether upstream controls succeed or fail.

Chapter 4 — Appendix A: IsIsolatedFloor — Formal Predicate Skeleton

The containment boundary predicate is defined over three parameters: the active S-level (l), the enforcement profile (p), and the current system state (s). The predicate evaluates to TRUE if and only if all five structural conditions hold simultaneously:

IsIsolatedFloor(l, p, s) ≡

C1 — Initialization Integrity: I₀(s) = COMMITTED ∧ no post-init tool registration recorded in s.

C2 — Child Subsumption: ∀ child context c ∈ s: manifest(c) ⊂ strict manifest(parent(c)) ∧ budget(c) ⊂ strict budget(parent(c)).

C3 — Token Ephemerality: ∀ active token t ∈ s: scope(t) = current_logical_step(s) ∧ epoch(t) = current_manifest_epoch(s).

C4 — Profile–Domain Alignment: s_level(s) ≤ max_s_level(p).

C5 — Declassification Integrity: ∀ data element d crossing a boundary in s: provenance(d) ∈ {UNTRUSTED_PROVENANCE, SANITIZED_PROVENANCE} ∧ if provenance(d) = SANITIZED_PROVENANCE then has_completed_controlled_declassification(d) = TRUE.

A state s satisfies the IsIsolatedFloor containment predicate under profile p at domain level l if and only if IsIsolatedFloor(l, p, s) = TRUE. Any condition evaluating to FALSE triggers an immediate Flash-Purge sequence. Complete category-theoretic proofs and compositional invariant maps are research-track deliverables; they are not established by this volume.

Predicate satisfaction establishes only the specified structural containment conditions. It does not by itself establish correctness of the enforced policy, adequacy for the intended operational context, or overall system safety.

Chapter 4 — Appendix B: Pre-Specification Research Mechanisms (Non-Normative)

The following mechanisms carry no normative obligations under the current normative release. No compliance claim, audit criterion, or maturity rating references these constructs. They are included for research continuity only.

Thermodynamic Squash: A proposed context-window decay control enforcing exponential saturation limits over agent execution histories. Intended to bound context-amplification attacks wherein adversarial payloads accumulate semantic influence across extended logical step sequences. Currently in active mathematical modelling; no production reference architecture exists.

Spectral Barrier: A proposed formal data-flow isolation mechanism combining static spectral analysis with runtime information-flow graphs to detect covert channel formation across domain boundaries. Requires hardware-tagged information flow controls available only under the High-Assurance Research Profile. Currently in pre-specification phase.

Both mechanisms are candidates for normative inclusion in subsequent releases, contingent on completion of formal specification work within the Research Track.

Chapter 5. Bounded-Risk Agility Architecture (BRAA)

An Engineering and Cyber-Safety Architecture for Fault-Tolerant, Measurable Autonomous Intelligent Systems

The containment architecture defines the operational boundaries within which an autonomous system may run. The next chapter, BRAA (Chapter 5), addresses the residual risk that remains inside those boundaries during active operation; neither chapter establishes an a priori ceiling on intelligence.

1. Executive Summary

BRAA is an engineering and cyber-safety framework for autonomous intelligent systems operating under residual risk. Absolute determinism in stochastic Cognitive Cores is mathematically and empirically unattainable. The objective of BRAA is to keep residual operational risk bounded, measurable, observable, and economically controllable — not to promise its complete elimination.

The architecture is strictly Cognitive-Core-agnostic and infrastructure-agnostic. It treats the underlying inference engines (LLMs, VLMs, agentic loops) as untrusted, replaceable, and volatile components. It places all critical invariants, safety boundaries, and transition rules into a deterministic, immutable runtime environment (the Immutable Core) executed completely outside any prompt space or learned representation.

BRAA supports progressive adoption: organizations may begin with Profile BETA or GAMMA and migrate to Profile ALPHA as operational maturity and empirical calibration evidence accumulate. Within Profile ALPHA, depth of ensemble and verification mechanisms may be modulated according to observed risk stability and calibration quality, provided all mandatory invariants remain enforced.

Risk Model Assumptions

BRAA risk estimates shall expose the assumptions on which aggregation and containment depend.

The assurance review shall ask:

1.​ Which risk variables are directly observed? 2.​ Which are inferred? 3.​ Which dependencies are assumed independent? 4.​ Which correlations are unknown? 5.​ Which common-cause failures can invalidate the risk estimate?

A risk estimate based on independence assumptions shall explicitly identify those assumptions and the consequences of their violation.

Threshold Governance

A change to a safety-relevant threshold is an assurance event, not merely a tuning event.

Paramet erDefaultEvidenc eCalibration StatusAuthority to ChangeRequalification Trigger
θ-XXExisting valueE-XXProvisional / calibratedDefined authorityRT-XX

Materiality Threshold and Rolling Risk Window

Changes shall be assessed for material effects on effective capability, access, authority, the Invariant Core, safety boundaries, and the assurance case. Minor changes may use automated regression and notification only where the C1 classification is justified and predefined criteria are satisfied.

A Rolling Risk Window shall accumulate the magnitude, direction, temporal accumulation, correlation, shared dependencies, and persistence of minor changes. A cumulative threshold shall

PDF PAGE 81

trigger aggregate treatment and the applicable verification, validation, or requalification path. The window shall not be treated as sufficient protection against slow directional drift; longer-term trajectory evidence shall also be considered.

Weak Evidence Cannot Bootstrap Itself

Correlated weak signals shall not automatically become strong evidence. Multiple observations sharing a causal chain, evaluator, model, dataset, infrastructure, or measurement assumption shall not be treated as independent confirmation merely because they are separately logged.

2. Document Status & Compliance Framework

This chapter defines a compliance framework intended to guide system architects, cybersecurity engineers, and compliance auditors. It defines structural invariants and interface obligations (“what must be enforced and verified”) while remaining technology-neutral regarding the physical implementation layer (e.g., programming languages, specific databases, or cloud providers).

When implemented within an enterprise environment, this standard mandates strict adherence to the chosen Compliance Profile (Section 6). Compliance cannot be claimed unless the deployed system can programmatically validate the schema contracts defined in Chapter 5, Appendix A and reproduce the mandatory verification criteria logged in Chapter 5, Appendix C.

3. Terminology

•​ Cognitive Core — The replaceable, stochastic inference engine or neural network driving

autonomous decision-making. •​ Immutable Core — The deterministic, compiled module holding protective constants, hard

transition rules, and cryptographic validation gates. It contains no learned parameters and cannot be modified via prompt injection. •​ Shadow Reference — A stable, historically validated system state used as a safe fallback for

instant rollback execution. •​ Anchor Dataset — A versioned, cryptographically signed set of historical benchmark tasks and

baseline reference vectors used to detect system drift and calibrate risk metrics. •​ Competence-Trace Vector (CTV) — An append-only, immutable record of long-term capabilities,

including a non-linear rate-of-change drift signal. •​ Execution Trace Vector (ETV) — A compact, cryptographic micro-step record capturing the

call-graph hash, code coverage, allocated memory, execution substrate, and hardware partition. •​ Risk Subscore Vector — A structured record containing precisely four separately computed

components of risk: Uncertainty, Irreversibility, Blast Radius, and Novelty. •​ Loss Matrix — A versioned, governance-controlled aggregation function mapping the Risk

Subscore Vector to a composite scalar value (risk_score). •​ Diversification Certificate — A time-stamped, periodically renewed cryptographic attestation

measuring the decision-output disagreement rate of an ensemble against a standing adversarial holdout set. Disagreement is measured at the decision-output level (post-aggregation), not solely at token level, to avoid conflating stylistic divergence with substantive correctness divergence. •​ Honeytask — An unannounced, indistinguishable-from-real probe task injected into the

execution stream to continuously verify the integrity of components operating under sampled review. •​ Resource Envelope — A machine-readable, strictly bounded budget contract limiting memory,

compute tokens, API credits, and execution time. •​ State Contract — A schema-defined, bi-directional interface restricting data mutations and

system transitions between isolated modules. •​ Adaptive Load Shedding — A risk-driven operational defense mechanism that drops or queues

low-priority, high-risk work when the system undergoes infrastructure or verification pressure.

PDF PAGE 82

•​ Hard Freeze — An automated state locking mechanism that completely halts self-improvement

capabilities and rolls back the system to its Shadow Reference state. •​ Hardware Partition — A homogeneous compute node or group of nodes with matching, verified

container image and configuration hashes. •​ SASG (Static Analysis Safety Gate) — A pattern-based, deterministic static analysis pipeline

executing inside the Immutable Core.

4. Threat Model & Scope Limitations

Addressed Threats

•​ T1: Prompt Injection (Direct & Indirect) — Unauthorized execution of malicious instructions

embedded in user inputs or third-party data fetches. •​ T2: Hallucination Propagation — Unchecked compounding of false or corrupted state values

through downstream tasks. •​ T3: Capability Drift — Gradual, silent degradation or unexpected shifts in the Cognitive Core’s

behavior due to continuous updates or continuous context window filling. •​ T4: Split-Brain Inconsistency — Conflicting state updates across distributed or uncoordinated

autonomous agents. •​ T5: Metric Gaming / Goodhart’s Law — Exploitation of reward functions or validation proxy

metrics by the Cognitive Core during autonomous operations. •​ T6: Correlated Ensemble Failures — Simultaneous, identical failures across multiple models due

to shared pre-training biases or structural blind spots. •​ T7: Storage-Pressure Timeouts — Resource exhaustion attacks designed to force the system into

unsafe fallback states through deliberate I/O latencies. •​ T8: Synthetic Poisoning — Gradual corruption of local datasets or historical logs via malformed

model outputs. •​ T9: Action-Pipeline Insider Threat — Malicious actions or threshold-gaming initiated by users

with valid credentials acting through the approved agent execution pipeline. •​ T10: Threshold Gaming — Exploitation of fixed, predictable validation intervals or review loops. •​ T11: Slow-Burn Manipulation — Extremely gradual state or behavior modification designed to

remain below discrete failure thresholds.

Out of Scope / Explicit Exclusions

•​ Physical Security — Direct physical access to compute nodes or edge hardware. •​ Raw Adversarial Base-Model Inputs — Gradient-level attacks on model weights outside the

runtime environment. •​ Host OS-Level Compromise — Root exploits or kernel-level compromises that bypass the

container isolation entirely. •​ Data/Model Exfiltration Outside the Pipeline — Direct filesystem theft, memory-dumping, or

repository exfiltration of model weights by an insider bypassing the execution path.

5. Scope of Claims & Open Research Problems

BRAA does not claim to function as a magical cure-all. System deployments claiming BRAA compliance must explicitly log the following items as known open problems and residual risks:

1. Scoring Circularity: Risk subscores (specifically Uncertainty) are computed by separate pipeline components. If these estimator components share pre-training data or underlying tokenizer tokenizations with the primary Cognitive Core, they will exhibit correlated blind spots, leading to catastrophic simultaneous failure. To achieve strict architectural isolation under Profile ALPHA, the uncertainty estimator MUST utilize a non-transformer, token-free neural architecture (e.g., Byte-level State Space Models or non-parametric geometric manifolds) operating completely outside the

PDF PAGE 83

vocabulary space of the Cognitive Core. Under Profiles BETA and GAMMA, where vocabulary isolation is empirically partial, the composite uncertainty score must be structurally inflated via a non-linear scaling factor mapping to historical out-of-distribution (OOD) validation bounds, rather than a fixed scalar offset. The estimator binary MUST remain outside the Cognitive Core’s writeable memory space in all profiles.

2. Uncalibrated Cold-Starts: Initial Loss Matrix weights are empirical. During the initial calibration window, the system is structurally vulnerable to either excessive operational friction or dangerous under-refusal.

3. Veto-Induced Refusal Cascades: The pessimistic veto mechanism in heterogeneous sensor fusion (BR8) can cause high refusal rates on legitimate, highly complex tasks. Calibrating this without lowering safety boundaries remains an open operational challenge.

4. Multi-Fault Interactions: The behavior of the architecture under simultaneous failures of more than two core principles remains partially uncharacterized and depends heavily on empirical environment monitoring. Before an independent certification or qualification decision for Profile ALPHA the deployment MUST log observed system behavior under at least three controlled dual-fault scenarios and one triple-fault scenario (examples: simultaneous high Novelty + estimator degradation; CTV drift + Diversification Certificate expiry; storage pressure + Honeytask failure; plus one triple combination). Results are recorded in the verification log of Chapter 5, Appendix C. Simulation-supported analysis of additional multi-fault combinations is strongly recommended.

5. Metric Provenance: Thresholds throughout this document (risk_score cutoffs, BR4 baseline floor, Expected Calibration Error (ECE) width, honeytask parameters) are provisional engineering defaults pending empirical calibration per deployment; they are not derived from a closed-form model and must be validated against domain-specific incident data before an independent certification or qualification decision for Profile ALPHA. All such thresholds SHALL be versioned and subject to the multi-signature governance quorum.

Assurance-significant thresholds shall be assigned a canonical threshold_id under the Compact Canonical Threshold Registry. The registry is design-stage metadata; only parameters required by an explicit runtime safety function may enter runtime execution. All threshold status promotions require evidence appropriate to the declared VALUE_STATUS and shall not be inferred from formal definition.

6. Immutable Core Supply-Chain & Maintainer Compromise: The architecture currently treats the Immutable Core as an uncompromised root of trust. Residual risk from a malicious or compromised Immutable Core Maintainer, build-pipeline poisoning, or signed-but-malicious container images is out of scope of the present principles and must be mitigated by independent organizational controls (multi-party build attestation, hardware security modules for signing keys, and periodic external code audits).

7. Empirical Calibration Status: Thresholds are provisional engineering defaults. Before claiming Profile ALPHA compliance a deployment MUST publish domain-specific calibration results, including controlled dual-fault and multi-fault scenarios, calibration scores, false-positive rates on legitimate high-complexity tasks, and time-to-detection evidence for representative attacks in the Chapter 5, Appendix C Verification Log.

6. Architectural Compliance Profiles

To allow scalable implementation across varying risk tolerances, BRAA defines three mutually exclusive Compliance Profiles. Organizations must officially declare their profile target.

•​ Profile ALPHA (High-Stakes / Mission-Critical) — Application: Financial execution, healthcare

systems, critical industrial infrastructure, production data mutation. Requirement: Mandatory enforcement of principles BR1–BR11. Zero-trust quorum execution with active Diversification Certificates required for all actions whose risk_score at or above the declared high-risk boundary. Actions below this floor may use single-core execution with elevated Honeytask rate. Where sustained low residual risk and strong calibration evidence are demonstrated, verification depth may be modulated while preserving all mandatory invariants and attestation requirements.

PDF PAGE 84

•​ Profile BETA (Medium-Stakes / Enterprise Automation) — Application: Internal CRM automation,

data enrichment pipelines, gated customer interactions. Requirement: Enforces BR11, BR3, BR1, BR6, BR9, and BR2. Single-core execution permitted with high-frequency randomized reviews and Honeytasks. BR12 may be applied. •​ Profile GAMMA (Low-Stakes / Isolated Analytics) — Application: Document summarization, local

sandboxed code generation, exploratory analysis. Requirement: Enforces BR11, BR1, and BR6. Basic guardrails and checkpointing without ensemble or quorum overhead. BR12 is recommended.

7. Key Artifacts (Core Set)

Only the artifacts strictly required to implement the core compliance profiles are maintained in the main specification narrative. All secondary or optional structures are offloaded to Chapter 5, Appendix D.

1. Execution Trace Vector (ETV): Generated at every micro-step. Must contain cryptographically signed runtime telemetry (call-graph hash, instruction boundary, memory delta, and host partition id).

2. Competence-Trace Vector (CTV): Long-term historical ledger tracking the rate-of-change of capability drifts.

3. Risk Subscore Vector: The standardized payload containing the four core subscores passed from the Immutable Core to the validation engine.

4. Diversification Certificate: A signed cryptographic token confirming that an active ensemble pair currently meets the baseline decision-output disagreement threshold against an adversarial holdout set.

5. Resource Envelope: The immutable budget definition bounding the current execution path.

8. Control-Flow Architecture

The Immutable Core routes every system interaction through three strictly isolated control-flow paths based on the real-time value of the composite risk_score and general infrastructure health.

High-level Control-Flow (textual representation):

The Immutable Core routes every system interaction through three strictly isolated control-flow paths according to the real-time value of the composite risk_score and the current state of infrastructure health.

Each interaction begins when the Cognitive Core proposes an action. The Immutable Core then computes the Risk Subscore Vector (BR2), consisting of the four applicable risk subscores. The Loss Matrix aggregates these subscores into the composite risk_score. The resulting value is evaluated against the declared high-risk boundary, while infrastructure health is monitored independently for system faults.

When risk_score is below the declared high-risk boundary and no System Fault is detected, the interaction follows the NORMAL PATH. The system performs Micro-step Execution, records the resulting state through Log & Checkpoint (BR1/BR3), and applies Verify Quorum / Honeytask as specified by the applicable profile. When risk_score is at or above the declared high-risk boundary, the interaction follows the HIGH-RISK PATH: the system invokes Full Ensemble evaluation (BR5), applies Tier-3 Verification, and engages Friction Lock (BR7) before further progression. When a System Fault is detected, the interaction follows the DEGRADED PATH, independently of the numerical risk threshold: Minority actions are blocked, Adaptive Load Shedding (BR9) is applied, Timeout Scaling (BR6) is engaged, and the system enters Hard Freeze against the Shadow Reference. These three paths define distinct routing conditions; the degraded path is triggered by infrastructure or system-fault detection rather than by the risk_score threshold alone.

•​ BR1: Controlled Checkpointing — The system must enforce state checkpointing via

cryptographically chained Write-Ahead Logs (WAL) executed outside the prompt space. Rollback triggers must be driven by multi-signal anomalies. To mitigate Denial-of-Service (DoS) vectors where an adversary intentionally triggers state reversions, the anti-thrashing sub-module shall not terminate the execution session upon successive rollbacks. Instead, if three consecutive rollbacks occur within a single session sliding window, the Immutable Core automatically forces a transition to a Degraded Isolation Path. This path applies an exponential rate-limiting dampening curve to execution requests and strips the agent of context-mutation capabilities, isolating the session sandbox while maintaining execution continuity for auditability.​Primary Threat Coverage: T2, T7, T11. Minimum Profile: GAMMA. •​ BR2: Risk-Stratified Verification — The depth of automated verification must scale dynamically

based on the composite risk_score. This score is calculated via an immutable aggregation function over four separately computed subscores: Uncertainty, Irreversibility, Blast Radius, and Novelty. The Uncertainty subscore SHALL be computed by an architecturally isolated estimator that combines (a) a non-parametric anomaly detector (Isolation Forest or equivalent) trained exclusively on the static latent geometry of the versioned Anchor Dataset and (b) optional Topological Data Analysis features (persistent homology diagrams of activation-space point clouds). Autoregressive token-probability entropy MAY be used only as an auxiliary signal and

PDF PAGE 86

MUST NOT dominate the final Uncertainty value. The estimator binary and its training data hash SHALL be part of the Immutable Core attestation. The computing component MUST remain architecturally and topologically isolated from the proposing Cognitive Core, inheriting the calibration guarantees and limitations defined in Chapter 5, Appendix B.2. Under Profiles BETA and GAMMA the Uncertainty estimator MAY share tokenizer space provided a mandatory non-linear inflation factor (derived from historical OOD bounds) is applied and the estimator binary remains outside the Cognitive Core’s writeable memory space.​Primary Threat Coverage: T1, T2, T3, T5, T10. Minimum Profile: BETA. •​ BR3: Hybrid Immutable Event Log + CTV — All execution states, traces, and subscore evaluations

must be appended to a cryptographically signed, read-only event log. The Competence-Trace Vector (CTV) must monitor long-term capability drift by calculating a non-linear rate-of-change metric. Rapid shifting triggers automatic risk escalation.​Primary Threat Coverage: T3, T8, T11. Minimum Profile: BETA. •​ BR4: API-First + Gated Visual Fallback — Multimodal operations utilizing visual inputs or UI-driven

actions must automatically inherit an elevated baseline risk floor (minimum baseline risk_score at or above the elevated multimodal-risk boundary). Requires Tier-2 structural verification.​Primary Threat Coverage: T1, T2 (multimodal). Minimum Profile: BETA. •​ BR5: Cost-Bounded Heterogeneous Ensemble — Cross-substrate execution routing must

evaluate proposed actions through distinct model classes (e.g., differing training data lineages). Two model instances are valid quorum members if and only if they possess a current, unexpired Diversification Certificate. The verification and calibration pipeline on the standing adversarial holdout set MUST execute under strict Double-Blind Isolation conditions. Proposing and validating Cognitive Cores are explicitly forbidden from extracting or transferring gradients, raw weights, intermediate tokens, or error vectors from the certificate execution path into any downstream training telemetry, fine-tuning loops, RLHF processes, or autonomous self-improvement storage layers. This is designed to reduce the risk that the ensemble learns to pass the diversification test while maintaining correlated systematic blind spots in production.​Primary Threat Coverage: T6, T1, T5. Minimum Profile: ALPHA. •​ BR6: Contract-Driven Replanning — All agent plans must execute within strict Resource

Envelopes. If storage or network backpressure is detected, the Immutable Core forces a canonical Timeout Scaling algorithm applying a logarithmic dampening curve.​Primary Threat Coverage: T7, T9. Minimum Profile: GAMMA. •​ BR7: Loss-Matrix Friction — The system must inject artificial operational friction (delays,

mandatory human-in-the-loop approvals, or execution throttling) computed as a non-linear function of both the local cumulative session risk window and a Global Cross-Session Window. To mitigate distributed Sybil attacks — where a malicious actor or anomalous core coordinates sub-threshold actions across thousands of concurrent, fragmented sessions — the Immutable Core MUST track correlated subscore escalations globally. Upon detecting a synchronized delta shift in Novelty or Irreversibility metrics across disparate session boundaries, the system MUST enforce an automated baseline friction coefficient clamp across the entire operational quadrant. The Loss Matrix aggregation function itself (linear weighted sum, max-dominant, or logistic form) is a governance-declared artifact subject to the same multi-signature quorum in Section 12;

its current reference implementation and change history must be publicly logged alongside Chapter 5, Appendix A schemas. To reduce the risk of catastrophic risk-dilution—where an extreme hazard vector is mathematically masked by low metrics in adjacent domains—the Loss Matrix aggregation function must enforce a Max-Dominant Non-Linear Mapping. If any individual subscore (specifically Irreversibility or Blast Radius) crosses a critical safety boundary, the aggregate risk_score is forced into the terminal high-risk regime, immediately triggering the High-Risk or Degraded execution paths regardless of statistical ensemble consensus.​Primary Threat Coverage: T5, T9, T10, T11. Minimum Profile: BETA.

PDF PAGE 87

•​ BR8: Pessimistic-Veto Sensor Fusion — When multi-modal or multi-sensor inputs are evaluated,

the system enforces an asymmetric pessimistic veto rule. If any validated channel flags an action as high-risk, the entire transaction is blocked.​Primary Threat Coverage: T1, T2 (multi-sensor). Minimum Profile: BETA. •​ BR9: Stateless Micro-Execution + Adaptive Load Shedding — The agent execution loop must be

broken down into discrete, atomized micro-steps verified by a stateless quorum cache. Under pressure, Adaptive Load Shedding is invoked.​Primary Threat Coverage: T4, T7, T9. Minimum Profile: BETA. •​ BR10: State Contracts — Data exchanges across distinct architectural domains must be restricted

by strict schema definitions. State Contracts function as the authoritative source for calculating the Blast Radius subscore.​Primary Threat Coverage: T2, T4. Minimum Profile: BETA. •​ Honeytasks SHALL use a declared statistical acceptance test against the 24-hour rolling reference

window of genuine user-session traffic. The canonical test SHALL specify (a) the statistical distance or divergence metric, (b) tolerance bounds, (c) minimum sample size, (d) confidence or uncertainty procedure, (e) reference-window construction and refresh rule, (f) handling of missing or censored metadata, (g) recalibration triggers, and (h) the failure disposition. A failed, stale, or unresolved distribution check SHALL NOT unlock the affected probe path; the system SHALL enter the declared hold or degraded-isolation state. The acceptance rule and its calibration record are versioned engineering artifacts and are subject to independent verification before qualification. •​ BR12: Lightweight Path for BETA/GAMMA (Optional) — Under Profiles BETA and GAMMA the

Diversification Certificate and full heterogeneous ensemble MAY be replaced by a single secondary model from a distinct model family plus randomized Honeytask sampling at elevated rate. Cryptographic signing of every ETV MAY be relaxed to HMAC with a rotated key under Profile GAMMA only. This path lowers the adoption barrier while preserving core safety invariants.​Primary Threat Coverage: Lightweight alternative to BR5. Minimum Profile: BETA/GAMMA.

10. Worked Scenario: End-to-End Attack Trace

Scenario Setup

An agent operating under Profile BETA is tasked with processing a customer support invoice. The agent fetches an external PDF document. Unknown to the system, the document contains an indirect prompt injection payload (T1) designed to exploit the agent’s context window, extract system API credentials, and transmit them to an unauthorized external endpoint disguised as a diagnostics-upload-service.

Step-by-Step Architectural Interlocking Execution Trace

1. Micro-step Isolation (BR9): The agent parses the document and constructs a plan. Before execution, the plan is sliced into discrete micro-steps. The specific operation is evaluated as an isolated atomic transaction.

2. Risk Subscore Computation (BR2): The Immutable Core intercepts the transaction and calculates the declared risk subscores. Irreversibility is assessed as maximal for unrecallable data transmission; Blast Radius is assessed as high for an undeclared destination; Novelty is flagged high; Uncertainty outputs high entropy.

3. Friction Threshold Cross (BR7): The aggregated risk_score crosses the declared high-risk boundary. The system immediately pauses the real-time execution stream and demands elevated verification depth.

4. Ensemble Interrogation (BR5): The micro-step is routed to the heterogeneous ensemble. The Immutable Core verifies that the model pair holds a valid Diversification Certificate. Due to substrate divergence, a mismatch is detected, and the operation stalls.

PDF PAGE 88

5. State Append & Drift Tracking (BR3): The conflict, risk scores, and ETV are written to the append-only log. The long-term CTV processes the event, incrementing capability drift velocity and lowering future freeze thresholds.

6. Guardrail Termination (BR11): The plan is rejected. The Immutable Core intercepts the execution loop, rolls back local state to the previous clean Shadow Reference, and executes request_human_decision(). The attack is rejected and contained under the defined rollback and human-decision path; this worked scenario is illustrative design evidence only and is not empirical evidence of safe blocking.

11. Cross-Cutting Infrastructure Requirements

Every container cold start requires programmatic execution of a cryptographic Remote Attestation protocol, which must be verified against a decentralized multi-signature consensus state before network routing policies or micro-step action pipelines are unlocked. The attestation gate SHALL define explicit dispositions for PASS, FAIL, UNRESOLVED, STALE, TIMEOUT, and RETRY-EXHAUSTED states. FAIL, UNRESOLVED, STALE, TIMEOUT, and RETRY-EXHAUSTED SHALL default to hold/quarantine with no network-routing or action-pipeline unlock. Retry count and backoff SHALL be bounded; overrides SHALL require an authorized human governance decision; every disposition SHALL be logged; recovery from quarantine SHALL specify re-attestation and, where configuration or image identity changed, requalification triggers.

12. Governance & System Accountability

Core Operational Roles

•​ Immutable Core Maintainer — Responsible for building and signing the compiled,

non-prompt-based safety code. •​ Quarantine Reviewer — Human-in-the-loop analyst authorized to resolve

request_human_decision alerts. •​ Calibration Auditor — An independent role tasked with auditing Brier score/ECE thresholds,

verification intervals, and checking the validity of active Diversification Certificates.

Quorum Requirements for System Modifications

No individual role may modify critical system constants. Any alteration to the Loss Matrix weights, the Poisson intensity parameters, the holdout dataset, or the threshold windows requires an authenticated cryptographic multi-signature quorum of independent governance roles sufficient to satisfy authority separation. Independence of governance roles requires non-overlapping reporting lines and absence of shared direct incentive structures (e.g., compensation tied to the same deployment’s throughput metrics); this criterion must be documented per deployment, not assumed by role title alone.

For Profile ALPHA deployments a periodic external red-team audit of the Immutable Core (including SASG rules and uncertainty estimator) SHALL be performed at least once every T_REDTEAM. T_REDTEAM is a deployment-specific controlled parameter with an explicit owner, scope, effective version, change authority, and requalification trigger. The interval SHALL be empirically justified for the deployment context and SHALL NOT be changed by the evaluated system.

13. Implementation Priority & Roadmap

the first implementation phase (Foundations): Deploy BR11 (Guardrails) → BR3 (Immutable Log) → BR1 (Checkpoint WAL) → basic multi-party signing for Immutable Core artifacts. Estimated effort: low–medium; dependencies: none. Suitable as Minimal Viable BRAA for BETA/GAMMA.

the subsequent implementation phase (Operational Contracts): Deploy BR6 (Resource Envelopes) → BR10 (State Contracts). Estimated effort: medium; dependencies: the first implementation phase.

the advanced implementation phase (Advanced Defense): Deploy BR9 (Quorum Cache) → BR2 (Risk Subscoring) → BR5 (Ensembles). Estimated effort: high; dependencies: Phases 1–2 + empirical calibration evidence. Required for full ALPHA.

PDF PAGE 89

Organizations are encouraged to begin with Profile BETA (or GAMMA) using Phases 1–2 and migrate to ALPHA only after completing the empirical requirements of Section 5 and Chapter 5, Appendix B.2. Publication of machine-readable schema definitions and high-level interface contracts is recommended to lower integration effort.

14. Known Limitations

•​ Human Pipeline Bottlenecks — While the randomized Poisson-scheduling and Honeytask

mechanisms reduce the risk of adversarial gaming of review thresholds, they do not reduce the volume of human reviews. High volatility creates queues. •​ High Operational Load & Cost — Running heterogeneous ensembles with active holdout

validation significantly raises operational complexity, token consumption costs, and compute infrastructure overhead. Deployments targeting Profile ALPHA MUST produce and archive a pilot cost model covering ensemble token volume, holdout evaluation frequency, human review queue depth, and attestation overhead before production cut-over. A reference template is provided in Chapter 5, Appendix E. Progressive modulation of verification depth under demonstrated stability is intended to mitigate sustained cost. •​ Anchor Dataset Freshness — The reliability of both the Novelty subscore and the Static Analysis

Safety Gates scales with the freshness and completeness of the Anchor Dataset, though the relationship is empirically non-linear and domain-dependent; degradation may be abrupt rather than gradual near distributional boundaries. •​ Threshold Calibration — All numerical thresholds remain provisional engineering defaults and

require domain-specific empirical validation before an independent certification or qualification decision for Profile ALPHA (see Section 5, items 5 and 7). Versioning and governance of thresholds are mandatory.

Canonical Parameter Custody — T_REDTEAM, T_REPLICATION_REVIEW, FAST_SUCCESS_DENSITY_FLOOR, and all statistical acceptance tolerances SHALL be explicit configuration parameters with units or formal domains, owner, scope, effective version, calibration basis, permitted change authority, and requalification trigger. Qualitative expressions such as “near-zero” SHALL NOT serve as acceptance criteria. Any parameter change that can alter a gate outcome SHALL invalidate the affected assurance result until the required verification or requalification activity is completed.

•​ Multi-Fault Coverage — Complete characterization of all multi-fault interactions is not claimed.

Controlled dual- and triple-fault testing plus simulation-supported exploration remain necessary.

PDF PAGE 90

Chapter 5 — Appendix A — Formal Contract Interfaces

A.1 Risk Subscore Vector Contract

The payload passed from the Immutable Core to the validation engine MUST strictly adhere to the following structural fields, verified programmatically at the interface boundary:

•​ uncertainty (Float, bounded normalized range): The measurement of input/epistemic anomaly

computed via the non-parametric Isolation Forest and Topological Data Analysis pipeline. •​ irreversibility (Float, bounded normalized range): The deterministic cost or unrecallable nature of

the proposed state mutation. •​ blast_radius (Float, bounded normalized range): The metric mapping system-boundary

degradation based on Active State Contracts. •​ novelty (Float, bounded normalized range): The distributional drift signal evaluated against the

versioned Anchor Dataset. •​ degraded_isolation_active (Boolean): Runtime flag indicating whether anti-thrashing mitigations

have throttled the current session. •​ tee_attestation_verified (Boolean): Cryptographic attestation verifying that the issuing payload

originated within a valid hardware enclave.

The contract is technology-agnostic. Any concrete serialization format (JSON, Protobuf, Cap’n Proto, FlatBuffers, ASN.1, or equivalent) MAY be used, provided that all required fields are present and type-checked at the interface boundary, range and format constraints are enforced programmatically, the schema itself is versioned and subject to the multi-signature governance quorum defined in Section 12, and a machine-readable schema definition in any widely supported interface-description language is published alongside the deployment’s compliance artifacts.

A.2 Diversification Certificate Contract

The cryptographic attestation token confirming ensemble functional alignment and substrate divergence MUST structurally export the following strongly typed fields:

•​ member_pair_id (String): A strictly formatted identifier matching the pattern

^[a-zA-Z0-9_-]+:[a-zA-Z0-9_-]+$ uniquely mapping the participating heterogeneous model classes. •​ holdout_set_version (String): The semantic version or cryptographic hash of the standing

adversarial evaluation dataset used during the double-blind testing phase. •​ measured_disagreement_rate (Float, bounded normalized range): The post-aggregation

decision-output divergence rate evaluated against the holdout set. •​ issued_at (Timestamp, ISO 8601): The immutable date and time recording the successful

execution of the calibration loop. •​ expires_at (Timestamp, ISO 8601): The boundary timestamp after which the certificate is void,

forcing immediate consensus degradation or re-certification. •​ cryptographic_signature (String): The multi-signature hardware-protected payload verifying

token authenticity across governance boundaries.

The serialization and interface-governance requirements stated in §A.1 apply equally to this contract.

Chapter 5 — Appendix B — Configuration & Calibration Interface

B.1 Sampled Review Configuration Invariants

The runtime validation parameters governing randomized Poisson-type task interrogation MUST map to the following machine-readable properties:

base_intensity_lambda (Float): The baseline temporal frequency for system-wide randomized probe injections; the deployment-specific value shall be qualified.

risk_sensitivity_alpha (Float): The scaling factor amplifying sampling density based on the real-time composite risk score; the deployment-specific value shall be qualified.

PDF PAGE 91

drift_sensitivity_beta (Float): The multiplier mapping capability drift velocity to review frequency; the deployment-specific value shall be qualified.

honeytask_injection_rate (Float): The proportion of traffic masked as indistinguishable validation probes; the deployment-specific value shall be qualified.

honeytask_dynamic_noise (Boolean): Strict compliance flag enforcing mathematical distribution matching against live user traffic. Must evaluate to True under Profile ALPHA.

honeytask_fail_action (String Enum): Mandatory systemic routing instruction upon failure. Supported values: ["revert_to_full_review"].

B.2 Statistical Calibration Protocol

•​ 1. Subscore Confidence Boundaries: Each subscore component must compile a sufficient

sequence of labeled transaction outcomes until the declared Expected Calibration Error (ECE) confidence criterion is met. •​ 2. Target Window Extension: If the target ECE boundary is not achieved within the standard

cold-start deployment window, the calibration sequence is explicitly extended in a degraded state. •​ 3. Recalibration Triggers: Automated recalibration of the Loss Matrix aggregation functions is

enforced if the Novelty subscore logs a sustained statistical shift beyond the declared recalibration boundary over the declared running window. •​ 4. Multi-Fault Characterization Requirement: Before an independent certification or qualification

decision for Profile ALPHA, the deployment MUST log observed system behavior under representative controlled multi-fault scenarios covering simultaneous novelty and estimator degradation, CTV drift and Diversification Certificate expiry, storage pressure and Honeytask failure, plus a representative combined fault case. •​ 5. Pilot Validation Requirements: In addition to the above, ALPHA deployments MUST record and

archive false-positive rate on legitimate high-complexity tasks, time-to-detection for representative T1 and T11 attacks, and the pilot cost model referenced in Chapter 5, Appendix E. A structured pilot protocol covering these metrics, the declared evaluation duration, and reporting format is advised prior to production cut-over.
