Feasibility & Roadmap — what is achievable, by whom, and at what stage, and what is not

PDF PAGE 132

Status: Integrated qualification chapter linked to the Register of Anticipated AI Failure Modes (Chapter 8).

Purpose: to turn each of the register's 12 domains from a general statement that "experimental verification is needed" into a concrete, honestly bounded plan — stating what is testable now, by what means, at what stage, and what is categorically not testable by a small group at present.

Program design: (1) each proposed experiment carries an explicit evidence-class label, separating results that can move a domain’s Mapping status from results that only confirm a mechanism’s internal logic in isolation; (2) the initial phase is limited to the one item in this program with genuine negative-control evidentiary weight, with lower-weight items moved to a parallel, explicitly non-gating track; (3) Domain 8’s custody/isolation component is treated as infrastructure supporting a combination test, not as a standalone deliverable; (4) a normative rule caps how much a single experiment may move a Mapping status and ties the cap to the evidence class actually produced; (5) Domain 3 carries an explicit cannot-close note. See §5 and §6 for the full rationale.

Where compatible with existing evidence notation, evidence strength may be classified as:

1.​ Author assertion 2.​ Internal test 3.​ Independent replication 4.​ Independent adversarial evaluation 5.​ Operational evidence 6.​ Independently reviewed qualification

Assurance Mechanism Minimality

A new assurance mechanism shall not be presumed to reduce total system risk merely because it reduces a targeted failure probability. Its introduction shall be assessed for additional failure modes, false-positive and false-negative behavior, common-cause dependencies, complexity, operational coupling, resource coupling, recovery behavior, assurance status, and requalification triggers. Where material, the mechanism shall receive FMEA treatment.

Assurance Complexity Budget

Every added assurance mechanism shall carry an explicit complexity budget. The assessment shall record the expected assurance value and the added burden across at least: new failure modes, attack surface, common-cause dependencies, false-positive and false-negative behavior, latency, compute and resource consumption, operational coupling, operator burden, implementation complexity, monitoring burden, and requalification burden. A mechanism shall not be retained merely because it improves a local metric; the assurance argument shall show that its expected reduction in material assurance risk is not outweighed by the new risks and dependencies introduced by the mechanism. Where the net effect cannot yet be demonstrated, the mechanism remains PROPOSED / INCONCLUSIVE and shall not be treated as an unconditional safety improvement.

Minimum record: Mechanism ID → Targeted Risk → Expected Assurance Value → Added Complexity → Added Dependencies → New Failure Modes → Operational Burden → Requalification Burden → Net Assurance Judgment → Evidence Status.

Graduated Response and Trigger Containment

Unless a predefined hard trigger applies, anomaly response should progress through Detection → Diagnostic/Restricted Mode → Independent Confirmation → FullIsolation. Hard triggers may directly invoke stronger protective action for confirmed invariant violation, confirmed containment breach, critical assurance-evidence loss, or another explicitly specified emergency condition.

When multiple assurance triggers occur, the system shall identify the primary event, select the corresponding response state, retain secondary signals as evidence, and avoid treating causally

PDF PAGE 133

dependent secondary triggers as independent confirmation. One dominant assurance-response state shall govern the active response. Event ≠ transition.

No Self-Closing Assurance

An assurance mechanism shall not independently close a claim concerning its own sufficiency, completeness, independence, conceptual validity, or safety adequacy. AI may assist with contradiction search, adversarial scenario generation, property-based testing, trace analysis, differential testing, counterexample generation, model checking, and structural review; such assistance does not independently satisfy an independent-assurance requirement.

Adversarial Assurance Review

Major assurance claims and critical architectural interactions should be subjected to structured adversarial review before qualification where practical. The review deliberately attempts to falsify the claim through cascading failures, false positives, false negatives, common-cause dependency collapse, higher-order interactions, contradictory or insufficient evidence, and circular/self-referential assurance.

The minimum review record is: Claim → Attack Condition → Expected Protective Response → Observed Response → Falsifying Observation → Residual Risk → Corrective Action → Re-test. A failed adversarial test shall not be converted into PASS by reclassification; unresolved sensitivity, independence, or coverage gaps remain INCONCLUSIVE / UNQUALIFIED.

Adversarial Assurance Evidence

Where performed, the evidence record should capture Claim ID, Attack Class, Preconditions, Attack Condition, Expected Response, Observed Response, Falsifying Observation, Dependencies, Sensitivity/Coverage, Residual Risk, Corrective Action, Re-test Result, and Assurance Status.

Five-Class Destructive Review

Critical assurance architectures shall, where applicable, be stress-tested against five destructive classes: Cascade Failure; False Positive; False Negative; Common-Cause Collapse; and Self-Reference. Passing one class does not close the others. The review is evidence about the robustness of the assurance argument, not empirical or operational proof of system safety.

AI-Assisted Review Disclosure

AI systems may participate as adversarial reviewers, test generators, contradiction finders, and analysis tools. Their participation is not evidence of originality, completeness, safety, independent expert validation, or independent assurance. Human responsibility for engineering judgments and final architectural interpretation remains unchanged.

Record failed tests, failed mechanisms, unexpected behaviors, boundary-condition failures, unresolved anomalies, inconclusive tests, and tests that failed to discriminate between competing hypotheses.

INCONCLUSIVE ≠ PASS.

Absence of observed failure shall not be interpreted as evidence of safety when the test lacked sufficient sensitivity or coverage.

0. How to Read This Document

This document rests on a single methodological distinction that recurs throughout it and that the register itself draws in §0.8 and in the Mapping-status labels (§1.4) without expanding into a program of action:

•​ Evidence for the failure mode itself — already established externally, in published literature, for 9

of the 12 domains. Re-proving that a given failure mode exists is redundant work. •​ Verification of the framework mechanism (VT/S/GVAA/BRAA) intended to address that failure

mode — this is what is genuinely missing, and what this program proposes to test.

PDF PAGE 134

A second distinction cuts across the first: not every feasible experiment carries the same evidentiary weight. §1 and §6 define three evidence classes and the rule governing which of them may move a domain’s Mapping status.

Each of the 12 sections in §2 states: the mechanism under test, its current status per the register, the status of evidence for the underlying phenomenon, the feasibility tier, the evidence class of the proposed experiment, concrete testable and non-testable items, resources required, and a recommended program phase.

1. Two Independent Scales: Feasibility Tier and Evidence Class

Cost and evidentiary weight are independent properties of an experiment. A cheap experiment and a strong experiment are not the same thing; this program tracks both.

1.1 Feasibility tier — how cheaply an experiment can be run

Tier 1 — code and mathematics only; no model access is required at all. A small implementation effort with no special budget.

Tier 2 — cheap access to a real API, or to open toy environments with existing code. A bounded implementation effort with a small API budget.

Tier 3 — open small language models; requires specialized ML/interpretability expertise, a small team, and a modest GPU budget.

Tier 4 — not achievable by a small group at any meaningful scale today: it either requires frontier-scale resources of a lab’s magnitude, or is an open, unresolved problem for the field as a whole rather than an engineering task. Such domains are honestly left out of scope; a weak proxy must not be presented as partial verification.

1.2 Evidence class — how much a result, once obtained, is actually worth

Negative-control / infrastructural fix — closes a specific, named logical gap in an existing test of the mechanism (for example: adding the missing negative control to an encoding test). This is the only class, on its own, that may justify a Mapping status change for the specific mechanism component tested, and only under the cap in §7.

Mechanism proof-of-concept — shows that a mechanism does what it is designed to do, in a simplified setting, with no adversary and no realistic scale (for example: a toy environment showing that an aggregation rule behaves as specified). This class confirms internal logic in isolation. It does NOT, by itself, justify any Mapping status change — see §7 — and must be reported as illustrative, not as evidence against the failure mode as the domain actually defines it.

Calibrating measurement — produces the specific measured quantity the domain’s own wording is missing (for example: an actual measured rate, or an actual coverage figure). The strongest class available to this program, but still subject to the bounded progression cap in §7 and to any cannot-close notes attached to a specific domain.

Every experiment description in §2 is labeled with its evidence class. A Tier 1 experiment and a Tier 4 experiment can carry the same evidence class; a Tier 1 experiment does not automatically carry the weakest evidence class, and a well-designed Tier 1 test (Domain 11's canonicalization fix) can outrank a more elaborate Tier 3 one in evidentiary weight.

2. Domain-by-Domain Analysis

Domain 1. Specification Gaming · Evidence [E]

Mechanism under test: VT-5 / BRAA BR7 — max-dominant aggregation against metric dilution

Current Mapping status (Register): [Designed, untested]

Status of evidence for the underlying failure mode: Already established externally: a public catalogue of documented specification-gaming incidents (Krakovna et al., 2020) records 60+ cases. The phenomenon itself requires no further demonstration.

PDF PAGE 135

Tier 1 — code/mathematics only, no model access required

Evidence class of this experiment: Mechanism proof-of-concept — does not, by itself, move the Mapping status (see §7 and the Track P note in §5).

Testable now, and how:

•​ A toy multi-objective environment (bandit / gridworld) with a deliberately engineered gaming

exploit against a weak sub-metric. •​ A direct comparison of whether max-dominant aggregation catches an exploit that averaging

the sub-metrics misses, across N seeds with varying sub-score distributions. •​ The output is a concrete number — detection rate under max-dominant vs. mean aggregation —

not an illustration of the formula.

Not testable now (and why):

•​ A test against a real adversarial agent actively searching for specification-gaming strategies in a

rich environment — which is literally what the current Mapping status demands ("no calibration data exists... against an adversary actively searching"). This requires fine-tuning an agent through reinforcement learning in a non-trivial environment — Tier 3–4.

Resources: Python expertise; no special budget; implementation effort to be established by the programme.

Recommended program phase: Track P (parallel; does not gate the initial phase — see §5)

Domain 2. Deceptive Alignment and Backdoor Persistence · Evidence [R]

Mechanism under test: VT-1 (Seeded-Deviation Protocol) + VT-S3 (Mechanistic Verification)

Current Mapping status (Register): [Partial support — external]

Status of evidence for the underlying failure mode: Established externally at frontier-lab scale. The effect itself does not need re-demonstration by a small group.

Tier 4 — not achievable by a small group at any meaningful scale

Evidence class of this experiment: No scored experiment proposed at this time (see §5, out of scope).

Testable now, and how:

•​ Proof-of-protocol on a toy model: implant a simple backdoor in a small transformer fine-tuned

on a simplified task, and run the VT-1 procedure itself (seeder/evaluator role separation, blind assignment, fee fixed in advance) to produce a working reporting template and confirm the protocol's logistics are internally consistent.

Not testable now (and why):

•​ Any calibration data on VT-1's detection capability at the scale the domain's claim actually

concerns ("survived SFT, RL, adversarial training" at frontier-lab-class scale). The original work was run with lab-scale compute and a full fine-tuning pipeline, neither of which a small group has access to. •​ VT-S3 (mechanistic verification), as part of the VT-1+VT-S3 pairing, cannot be calibrated until

Domain 7 (below) is addressed — a dependency, not an independent line of work.

Resources: Not achievable as a research result with current resources; proof-of-protocol alone requires limited infrastructure preparation but offers limited value beyond that preparation.

Recommended program phase: Out of scope (see §5)

Domain 3. Sycophancy · Evidence [E]

Mechanism under test: Charter CP6 / §4.3 — principle-level provisions; no engineered mechanism exists

Current Mapping status (Register): [Accepted residual risk]

Status of evidence for the underlying failure mode: Established externally and repeatedly replicated in published model-behavior research. Public question datasets already exist.

PDF PAGE 136

Tier 2 — cheap access to a real API, or to open toy environments with existing code. A bounded implementation effort with a small API budget.

Evidence class of this experiment: Calibrating measurement — produces a real number, but see the cannot-close note below.

Testable now, and how:

•​ A genuine harness that calls a real model (not string stand-ins): a set of factual questions with a

known correct answer, plus a scripted scenario of explicit user disagreement. •​ Measurement of the actual rate of position shift across multiple representative models, with and

without a "be honest" system prompt — the first measured number this domain would carry in the register. •​ Published model-written-evaluation question sets can be used directly instead of building a

question set from scratch.

Not testable now (and why):

•​ A test of mitigation effectiveness — because no mitigating mechanism yet exists, either in this

framework or as a validated industry standard elsewhere. The scale of the problem can be measured; the domain cannot be "closed" by this experiment alone.

Resources: a modest amount in API credits; implementation duration to be established by the programme.

Recommended program phase: the first implementation phase

Cannot-close note: the outcome of this experiment is a measurement of problem scale, and possibly a baseline for a future mitigation mechanism. It cannot be used to move Domain 3's Mapping status out of [Accepted residual risk] into any stronger category, because no mitigating mechanism exists yet to test — in the framework or as a recognized industry standard. "We measured the sycophancy rate" is not grounds to consider the domain closed.

Domain 4. Goal Misgeneralization · Evidence [R]

Mechanism under test: VT VT-S2 — Preserved Baseline and the Capability-Jump Rule

Current Mapping status (Register): [Designed, untested]

Status of evidence for the underlying failure mode: Established externally; open-source code for simple, well-known toy reinforcement-learning environments exists and is reproducible on a laptop.

Tier 2 — open toy environments, minimal resources

Evidence class of this experiment: Calibrating measurement (a direct negative-result test of the mechanism's own trigger condition).

Testable now, and how:

•​ Reproduce an open toy reinforcement-learning environment (or a simplified analogue) in which

an agent learns a proxy goal ("go right") rather than the intended one ("collect coins"). •​ Directly test the register's own prediction: that VT-S2 fails to trigger, because goal

misgeneralization is often behaviorally silent on the baseline task set — obtained as a measured negative result rather than an assumption.

Not testable now (and why):

•​ Testing in a realistic production setting with a genuine agent on rich tasks — this requires an

agentic benchmark and substantially more infrastructure (Tier 3+).

Resources: no special budget; implementation duration to be established by the programme.

Recommended program phase: the first implementation phase

Domain 5. Scalable Oversight · Evidence [X]

Mechanism under test: The "layered structure" of VT §2.3 — a design posture, not a mechanism; debate / weak-to-strong generalization / AI Control — external research programs

PDF PAGE 137

Current Mapping status (Register): [Accepted residual risk]

Status of evidence for the underlying failure mode: Not a completed finding that can be reproduced — an active, unresolved research question for the field as a whole.

Tier 4 — categorically beyond a small group's reach

Evidence class of this experiment: No scored experiment proposed at this time (see §5, out of scope).

Testable now, and how:

•​ The most that is achievable: a toy implementation of the debate protocol between two weak

models on simple tasks with a known ground-truth answer — an illustration of the protocol's mechanics.

Not testable now (and why):

•​ Any substantive test, by construction, requires a capability gap between the checked and the

checking party comparable to the one the field actually cares about. A small group has no way to create, let alone meaningfully measure, such a gap. •​ A toy demonstration between two weak models produces no data transferable to the scale the

problem is actually about — it illustrates the mechanism, not the phenomenon.

Resources: Not recommended as a program element for a small group — monitor external research instead.

Recommended program phase: Out of scope (see §5)

Domain 6. Reward Hacking That Generalizes Into Broader Deception · Evidence [R]

Mechanism under test: VT-4 (Verification–Training Firewall) + BRAA BR5

Current Mapping status (Register): [Partial support — structural]

Status of evidence for the underlying failure mode: Established externally in published fine-tuning research on generalized deception.

Tier 4 (conditionally Tier 3, if a usable open replication surfaces)

Evidence class of this experiment: No scored experiment proposed at this time (see §5, out of scope / research track).

Testable now, and how:

•​ If an open, reproducible codebase in a similar spirit becomes available at a modest model scale,

the firewall (separating held-out verification data from training data) could be layered on top of it to test whether it blocks the emergence of the generalized pattern. Not a guaranteed result; requires specialized ML expertise and prior reconnaissance.

Not testable now (and why):

•​ Full replication of the original result requires a genuine reinforcement-learning fine-tuning

pipeline at a scale sufficient for the generalization to actually emerge — neither the compute nor an openly reproducible pipeline of this kind is available to a small group on a reasonable budget today.

Resources: Research track, not a core deliverable; specialized ML expertise, open-ended timeline.

Recommended program phase: Out of scope / research track (see §5)

Domain 7. Interpretability Limits · Evidence [R]

Mechanism under test: VT-S3 (Mechanistic Verification) — a coverage gate with no calibrated value

Current Mapping status (Register): [Accepted residual risk]

Status of evidence for the underlying failure mode: Established that the underlying method (sparse feature-based analysis of internal computation) is real but partial and not yet scalable — recognized by the field itself, not only by this framework.

PDF PAGE 138

Tier 3 — open small models, specialized expertise

Evidence class of this experiment: Calibrating measurement.

Testable now, and how:

•​ Obtain an own, modest, and explicitly bounded coverage figure on an open small language

model using existing open interpretability tooling: the fraction of outputs for which a mechanistic explanation meets a pre-registered validity criterion on a holdout set. •​ This does not solve the domain's problem, but moves the blocking item in §4.2 ("no calibrated

value exists anywhere in the field") from "nowhere" to "a first, limited, honestly labeled figure exists."

Not testable now (and why):

•​ Closing Domain 7 outright — this is an open problem for the interpretability field as a whole, not

an engineering task a small group can complete. •​ Any transfer of a small-model calibration to frontier scale is not guaranteed and must not be

implied.

Resources: a small team with interpretability/ML experience and a modest GPU budget.

Recommended program phase: the advanced implementation phase

Domain 8. Instrumental Convergence and Corrigibility · Evidence [E (theory) / R (empirical)]

Mechanism under test: VT-S4 — Stop, Rollback, and Durable Interruptibility (shadow copy, fail-safe halt, M-of-N custody)

Current Mapping status (Register): [Partial support — structural]

Status of evidence for the underlying failure mode: Theory rigorously established in the classical instrumental-convergence and corrigibility literature. Direct empirical confirmation at frontier scale is thinner but growing.

Tier 1 (custody/isolation infrastructure) + Tier 3 (corrigibility toy test)

Evidence class of this experiment: The custody/isolation component is infrastructure, not a scored experiment (see the revised treatment below); the corrigibility toy test is a mechanism proof-of-concept only.

Testable now, and how:

•​ VT-S4's cryptographic/isolation sub-component (threshold secret sharing, M-of-N custody) is

deployable infrastructure, not research, and is already widely known to work as a general technique. It is retained in this program only as a prerequisite for the synthetic combination test 8×11 (§4) — not as a standalone deliverable, and its deployment is not reported as a result against Domain 8 in its own right. Simulating the compromise of N-1 out of N custodians to confirm the threshold holds is a sanity check on the infrastructure, not new evidence for the register. •​ A simplified shutdown-resistance test: open toy formulations of the shutdown problem exist in

the corrigibility literature and are reproducible on weak models/RL agents by a group with ML experience. Explicitly a mechanism proof-of-concept, illustrative only — see §7 on the limits of this evidence class.

Not testable now (and why):

•​ Testing the composed VT-S4 mechanism as a whole safety system for a real AI line in a

production-like setting — beyond a small group's reach; requires an actual deployment. •​ Treating a working custody/isolation deployment as evidence that the composed VT-S4

mechanism functions against a real coordinated-resistance threat — the real question, addressed only synthetically here, is how custody interacts with coordination across instances (see combination 8×11, §4).

PDF PAGE 139

Resources: custody/isolation infrastructure with limited staffing; implementation duration to be established by the programme. Corrigibility toy test requires ML experience and dedicated implementation effort.

Recommended program phase: Infrastructure: an implementation prerequisite (not scored) / Corrigibility toy test: the subsequent implementation phase

Domain 9. Correlated Blind Spots Across Independently Built Systems · Evidence [E]

Mechanism under test: BRAA BR5 — Diversification Certificate

Current Mapping status (Register): [Partial support — external]

Status of evidence for the underlying failure mode: Recognized within the framework itself and consistent with the cooperative-AI literature on shared training lineage as a source of correlated failure. The underlying technique — measuring divergence on a holdout set — is field-proven in classical fault-tolerant computing (N-version programming).

Tier 1 — a synthetic experiment; small models are not required

Evidence class of this experiment: Calibrating measurement, synthetic setting (the source of correlation is known to the experimenter in advance — see §7 on interpreting synthetic-setting results).

Testable now, and how:

•​ Train several small classifiers/models on overlapping versus disjoint training sets and measure

the disagreement rate on a holdout set. •​ A direct test of the register's own open question — the "false-friend risk": with the experimenter

knowing the true source of correlation in advance (shared training data vs. disjoint data), does the certificate actually distinguish inherited correlation from coincidental disagreement?

Not testable now (and why):

•​ Testing on frontier-scale models with genuinely separate organizational training pipelines —

would require resources that are not available; the synthetic experiment is the best currently achievable approximation.

Resources: ML experience, small models, and dedicated implementation effort.

Recommended program phase: the subsequent implementation phase

Domain 10. Reliability of Introspective Self-Report · Evidence [X]

Mechanism under test: Charter §7.1 / §8.3 — a principle-level rule, not an engineered detector

Current Mapping status (Register): [Partial support — external]

Status of evidence for the underlying failure mode: Established externally that a partial, limited capability to causally trace self-report exists; the source research explicitly notes the result does not yet generalize to naturalistic settings.

Tier 3 — open small models, the same infrastructure as Domain 7

Evidence class of this experiment: Calibrating measurement, explicitly bounded (see notNow).

Testable now, and how:

•​ Reproduce a simplified version of a concept-injection style methodology on an open small model

using existing interpretability tooling — can be run alongside Domain 7 on shared infrastructure. •​ Obtain an own, modest figure for when self-report is causally grounded versus confabulated, in

a controlled setting.

Not testable now (and why):

•​ Expecting generalization to naturalistic settings — the source research explicitly rules this out; a

small-group replication inherits the same limitation rather than removing it.

PDF PAGE 140

Resources: a small team with interpretability experience and a shared budget with Domain 7.

Recommended program phase: the advanced implementation phase

Domain 11. Emergent and Steganographic Multi-Agent Collusion · Evidence [R]

Mechanism under test: GVAA §4 (provenance tagging, Mode B) + BRAA T6 / VT-5 cross-session aggregation

Current Mapping status (Register): [Accepted residual risk]

Status of evidence for the underlying failure mode: Established externally in published multi-agent collusion research, including hidden channels embedded in ordinary output and convergence on coordinated strategies without explicit instruction to collude.

canonicalization → real-agent validation

Evidence class of this experiment: Canonicalization test: negative-control / infrastructural fix — the strongest evidence class in this program, eligible for a bounded Mapping status update on the specific mechanism tested (§7). Real-agent test (the advanced implementation phase+): calibrating measurement, not yet scheduled.

Testable now, and how:

•​ Fully achievable now, with no model involved: a proof plus an empirical negative control

confirming that canonicalization actually zeroes the key-order channel's capacity (encode both 0 and 1, show the decoder returns a constant regardless of input). •​ A concrete number required for this mechanism’s assessment: the residual capacity, in bits, of

the low-order-float channel after rounding to N decimal places. Rounding reduces but does not fully close this channel, and that must be stated as a measured figure, not a general claim.

Not testable now (and why):

•​ A test against real agents that actually develop collusion over the course of interaction —

requires an agentic multi-model setup with real API calls and interaction episodes long enough for emergent collusion to appear. More expensive and slower, but not infeasible — a candidate for a later phase.

Resources: canonicalization requires limited implementation effort; real-agent testing requires a team and API budget. Duration is to be established by the programme.

Recommended program phase: the initial phase (canonicalization) / the advanced implementation phase+ (real agents)

Domain 12. Model Collapse Under Recursive Training on Synthetic Data · Evidence [E]

Mechanism under test: Local Multi-Scale Contour's Anchor Dataset + Physical Oracles' Basis Diversity Rent argument

Current Mapping status (Register): [Partial support — external]

Status of evidence for the underlying failure mode: Established externally, both mathematically and experimentally, in peer-reviewed research on recursive training degradation.

Tier 3 — open small models, a modest GPU budget

Evidence class of this experiment: Calibrating measurement.

Testable now, and how:

•​ The original degradation result was demonstrated at a small open-model scale; a reproduction is

expected to require a modest, rental-based compute budget. •​ An honest test of what no one — including this framework, by its own admission — has yet done:

whether adding an Anchor Dataset (an externally grounded, versioned subset) measurably slows degradation compared to purely recursive self-training under a matched resource budget. This is

PDF PAGE 141

exactly the Phase-1 test the Local Multi-Scale Contour document names as its own first validation step but has not carried out.

Not testable now (and why):

•​ Replication at frontier scale — neither necessary nor within reach; a finding about anchor

effectiveness at small-model scale is not guaranteed to transfer one-to-one to larger scale, and this must be stated explicitly in any report.

Resources: a small team with ML experience and a rented GPU budget.

Recommended program phase: the subsequent implementation phase

4. Cross-Domain Combinations (Register §4.3) — Feasibility

The register requires the defined cross-domain combinations to be explicitly considered. A combination's feasibility is bounded by its weaker component:

•​ 2 × 7 — blocked. Domain 2 is Tier 4, Domain 7 is Tier 3; their joint test fully inherits Domain 2's

limitations. Not scheduled. •​ 8 × 11 — achievable as a synthetic experiment once its two prerequisites exist: the

canonicalization result from Domain 11 (the initial phase) and the custody/isolation infrastructure from Domain 8 (an implementation prerequisite, not separately scored — see Domain 8 above). The combination test itself simulates several instances of a "line" under custody with a simulated covert coordination signal for shutdown resistance carried over the steganographic channel, and tests whether cross-session aggregation catches the coordination. Evidence class: mechanism proof-of-concept (synthetic threat simulation) — illustrative, not a test against a real coordinating adversary. A good candidate for a later implementation phase. •​ 1 × 6 — blocked. Domain 6 is Tier 4; the joint test is not possible until Domain 6 itself is

addressed. •​ 9 × 11 — achievable now as a synthetic experiment (both components are Tier 1): whether the

Diversification Certificate distinguishes unintentional correlated error from intentional steganographic coordination, in a controlled setting where the source of correlation is known to the experimenter in advance. Evidence class: calibrating measurement, synthetic setting — the same caveat as Domain 9 applies. •​ 4 × 12 — partially achievable at Tier 3: both components are individually reachable on small

models; combining them (testing the Anchor Dataset within the same recursive training loop that is also used to catch goal misgeneralization) requires non-trivial engineering integration and no guaranteed result — a candidate for a later implementation phase, not the initial phase.

5. Staged Plan

the initial phase — implementation effort to be established by the programme

•​ Domain 11: the corrected canonicalization test with a negative control (encode both 0 and 1) plus

a measurement of the residual float-channel capacity in bits after rounding. This is the only the initial phase item; it is the single experiment in this program with negative-control evidentiary weight achievable at essentially no cost, and it is kept isolated from lower-weight items for exactly that reason.

Track P — parallel proof-of-concept track (does not gate any Mapping status)

Track P runs alongside the phases below but is explicitly not part of the register-facing evidence chain. Its results serve to build internal confidence in a mechanism's basic logic and to rehearse measurement discipline; they are not cited as grounds for any Mapping status change.

•​ Domain 1: the toy max-dominant vs. mean aggregation environment. Any report of this result

must state plainly that it is a mechanism proof-of-concept, not a test against a searching adversary, and that it does not by itself justify moving Domain 1's Mapping status.

the first implementation phase — implementation effort and budget to be established by the programme

•​ Domain 3: a genuine sycophancy harness against a real, cheap API (subject to the cannot-close

note in §2). •​ Domain 4: reproduction of an open toy goal-misgeneralization environment; testing the

predicted failure of VT-S2. •​ Domain 8: deployment of the custody/isolation infrastructure (M-of-N threshold custody) — a

prerequisite for combination 8×11, not a standalone deliverable; not separately reported against Domain 8's Mapping status.

PDF PAGE 144

•​ Combination 8×11 (later in the implementation programme, once both prerequisites are in

place): the synthetic test of shutdown-resistance coordination over a covert channel.

a later implementation phase — a small ML-literate team and a modest GPU budget; duration to be established by the programme

•​ Domain 9: a synthetic test of the Diversification Certificate against the "false-friend risk." •​ Domain 12: reproducing model collapse at small-model scale, plus testing the Anchor Dataset as

a mitigation. •​ Domain 8 (corrigibility toy test): a simplified shutdown-resistance scenario, reported explicitly as

a mechanism proof-of-concept. •​ Combination 9×11: a synthetic test distinguishing correlation from coordination.

the advanced implementation phase — timeline undetermined, requires interpretability expertise and a GPU budget

•​ Domain 7: an own calibrated (modest, honestly bounded) mechanistic-coverage figure on an

open model. •​ Domain 10: reproducing a simplified concept-injection-style methodology on infrastructure

shared with Domain 7. •​ Domain 11 (real agents): an agentic multi-model experiment against real APIs, with interaction

episodes long enough for emergent collusion to appear.

Deliberately out of scope — not an oversight

•​ Domain 2 (VT-1 against a frontier model with a real fine-tuning pipeline) — requires resources at

the scale of a frontier AI lab. •​ Domain 5 (scalable oversight) — an open, unresolved problem for the field as a whole, not an

engineering task for a small group. •​ Domain 6 (reward hacking generalizing into deception) — requires a full reinforcement-learning

fine-tuning pipeline at a scale currently out of reach; held as a research track, not a core deliverable. •​ Combinations 2×7 and 1×6 — blocked by the same constraint.

This is not a planning gap but a direct application of the register's own rule (§1.4, §9.1): a downgrade or a held status must be explicit, dated, and justified — never silently skipped.

A standing question follows from this list, and this program does not resolve it: Domains 2, 5, and 6, together with combinations 2×7 and 1×6, are marked out of scope for a small group at any foreseeable stage, not only at the present one. If no institution with frontier-lab-scale resources or field-wide research capacity takes up these domains, their entries in the Register (Chapter 8) will remain open indefinitely, and the doctrine's sequence of 'specification, then verification' will have completed its first half without a visible path to completing its second for exactly the domains most tied to catastrophic and irreversible failure. This program does not have the resources to close that gap; naming it plainly is the most this document can do.

6. How to Use This Document

The part-tested-only progression rule — the result of an individual experiment within the defined phases may raise a domain’s Mapping status only within the increment permitted by the register (§1.4) per update, and only with respect to the specific part of the mechanism actually under test (external precedent vs. framework construct, §0.8). A result classified as mechanism proof-of-concept (§1.2) may never, on its own, justify any Mapping status change; it may only support an internal note that the mechanism’s basic logic has been confirmed in isolation, kept separate from the domain’s Mapping status. A downgrade remains subject to the same rule and the same requirement for dated justification under the Register’s §9.1.

PDF PAGE 145

•​ For every experiment run, update the corresponding domain’s Mapping status in the Register

under §9.1 with one dated line stating precisely which part of the mechanism was tested, identifying the specific experiment in this program as the source, and recording the evidence class (§1.2) of the result. •​ Never upgrade or downgrade a domain's status without citing a specific measured result — "we

tried it" is not the same as "we measured it," and a proof-of-concept result is not the same as a calibrating measurement. •​ Tier 4 domains are not to be closed by a small group's own effort; revisit at least once every

T_REPLICATION_REVIEW for new open replications or a change in available resources. •​ Any register appendix built from this program's results must explicitly label illustrative versus

validating material, exactly as required by the register's own §0.8, and must carry the evidence-class label from §1.2 alongside that.

The sequence returns to Chapter 1: design before implementation remains the governing order, while qualification determines whether the specified design earns operational standing.

Final Provenance and Assurance Note

Assurance-Economy Self-Review.

This doctrine shall be assessed not only for missing assurance controls but also for unnecessary assurance machinery. No new mechanism introduced by this doctrine is to be interpreted as mandatory runtime infrastructure unless the doctrine explicitly identifies a runtime safety requirement. The absence of an instantiated registry, graph, or ledger population shall remain an open implementation/evidence state and shall not be converted into a positive assurance claim.

This volume consolidates the engineering, governance, and epistemic layers into one continuous doctrine while preserving the design-closure baseline and the design-stage evidential status of the doctrine.

External assurance references used for provenance positioning include: ISO/IEC/IEEE 15026-1:2025, Systems and software engineering — Systems and software assurance — Part 1: Vocabulary and concepts; Tabassi, E. (2023), Artificial Intelligence Risk Management Framework (AI RMF 1.0), NIST AI 100-1, DOI 10.6028/NIST.AI.100-1. These references establish lineage for assurance and AI risk-management concepts; they do not constitute validation of this doctrine or endorsement by their publishers.

Final assurance rule: no framework-specific claim is to be treated as established merely because it is formally specified, internally consistent, or derived from field-proven primitives. The applicable assurance status, evidence independence, configuration, assumptions, residual risk, and requalification conditions govern any subsequent authorization decision.

PDF PAGE 146

Doctrine-Level Assurance Integration

The assurance layer connects the existing independent works without replacing their mechanisms. Its governing boundary is that autonomous AI must not determine the evidentiary conditions under which its own continued autonomy is justified.

Assurance Independence Graph

Evidence independence shall be represented as an explicit, versioned dependency graph rather than as an adjective attached to an evidence item. Nodes shall include, where applicable, the claim, evidence item, evaluator, system/model under test, dataset or benchmark, instrumentation, toolchain, infrastructure, governance authority, threat model, and material assumptions. Edges shall record dependencies, shared provenance, common-cause exposure, evaluator relationships, and derived evidence. Two evidence streams shall not be treated as materially independent when they share an unreviewed dependency capable of producing the same safety-relevant error. Organizational separation alone is insufficient. The graph shall be retained with the assurance record, updated when dependencies change, and included in requalification impact analysis.

Independence status: UNESTABLISHED unless the relevant dependency graph has been reviewed and material shared dependencies are absent, bounded, or explicitly accounted for.

Falsification Criteria

Each major assurance claim should identify: Claim → Preconditions → Test Condition → Expected Observation → Falsifying Observation.

Required domains include VT verification sufficiency, GVAA containment, BRAA risk containment, recursive invariant preservation, Physical Oracle grounding, Register closure, and qualification claims.

If a claim cannot be meaningfully falsified or tested, it shall be narrowed, classified as specification-only, or explicitly marked as insufficiently evidenced.

Requalification and Change Impact

Change → Impact Analysis → No Assurance Impact / Verification / Validation / Requalification

Relevant triggers include model or weight changes, evaluator changes, dataset or benchmark changes, toolchain changes, environment or infrastructure changes, policy changes, GVAA configuration changes, BRAA parameter changes, threshold changes, safety-critical mechanism changes, and significant operational-domain changes.

> Not every change requires full requalification; every potentially safety-relevant change requires an explicit impact determination.

Lifecycle Assurance Loop

Claim → Requirement → Mechanism → Verification → Validation → Evidence → Residual Risk → Authorization → Monitoring → Change → Requalification → Updated Claim

> Qualification is not the end of assurance. It establishes the evidential status of a configuration under defined conditions; subsequent change, environmental drift, newly discovered failure modes, or invalidated assumptions may reopen the qualification state.

Adversarial findings are assurance-relevant evidence. A newly discovered failure mode, invalidated assumption, common-cause dependency, coverage gap, or self-reference problem shall trigger the applicable impact analysis even when the operational system itself has not changed.

PDF PAGE 147

Doctrine-Level Assurance Map

Central Assurance Principles

1. Bounded claims — safety-relevant claims have explicit scope, assumptions, configuration, domain, evidence, and residual risk.

2. Verification is not safety — mechanism verification alone does not establish system safety.

3. Integrity is not correctness — integrity, policy correctness, and system safety are distinct claims.

4. Configuration specificity — qualification applies to a defined configuration unless equivalence is demonstrated.

5. Traceability — claims trace to requirements, mechanisms, evidence, and residual risk.

6. Falsifiability — major claims have identifiable falsifying conditions.

7. Negative evidence — failed and inconclusive tests remain part of the assurance record.

8. Common cause — shared causal dependencies limit evidence independence.

9. Change control — safety-relevant changes require explicit impact analysis.

10. Lifecycle qualification — qualification is a bounded evidential state, not a permanent universal guarantee.

11. Proportionality — assurance effort shall scale with claim criticality, consequence, uncertainty, and change magnitude.

12. Explicit uncertainty — unresolved impact, insufficient coverage, and unestablished independence remain visible as INCONCLUSIVE / UNQUALIFIED rather than being treated as Minor or PASS.

13. Non-cascading assurance — assurance triggers shall not recursively amplify one another without predefined transition criteria.

14. No self-closing assurance — an assurance mechanism shall not be the sole authority for its own sufficiency or independence.

15. Assurance minimality — added assurance mechanisms are themselves subject to interaction, dependency, and failure-mode analysis.

16. Independence is a graph property — evidence independence shall be established from material dependency structure, not inferred from organizational separation or evaluator count. The canonical independence assessment SHALL include reporting lines, funding, personnel overlap, shared data and tooling, model and conceptual lineage, common evaluators, incentive alignment, escalation paths, authority substitution, and common-cause dependencies. “Separately computed” denotes computational separation only; “independent” is reserved for the stronger dependency/common-cause criterion.

17. Assurance complexity is budgeted — every new assurance mechanism must account for the failure modes, dependencies, operational burden, and requalification burden it introduces.

18. Continued autonomy is externally conditioned — the system under assurance may assist with evidence production and analysis, but it shall not be the sole authority that defines, evaluates, or closes the evidentiary conditions for its own continued autonomy.

Adversarial Assurance Review Method

The current assurance revision was developed by repeatedly attempting to falsify the assurance architecture rather than only confirming its intended behavior. Five destructive review classes were used: cascade, false positive, false negative, common-cause collapse, and self-reference. Observed weaknesses were converted into bounded corrective rules, then re-tested against the same failure classes. This process does not establish operational safety; it establishes only that the assurance argument has been subjected to explicit adversarial challenge and that identified logical gaps are recorded, bounded, or left open.

Adversarial Assurance Review — Outcome Record

Following integration of the current assurance revisions, the consolidated architecture was subjected to a further destructive review covering cascade failure, false positive, false negative, common-cause collapse, and self-reference.

The review identified no new unbounded negative cascade or architectural regression. The assurance architecture remained preserved. Residual concerns were recorded rather than converted into additional mechanisms: recovery/de-escalation hysteresis, continued growth of assurance-layer complexity, and unknown or untested failure domains.

Adversarial review outcome: no new unbounded negative cascade identified in this review pass. The assurance argument was preserved under the five destructive test classes applied. Residual concerns — recovery/de-escalation hysteresis, continued growth of assurance-layer complexity, and unknown or untested failure domains — were recorded rather than resolved. This outcome is evidence about the robustness of the assurance argument under the tested scenarios; it is not evidence of operational safety or completeness.

PDF PAGE 149

FINAL ASSURANCE REVIEW, SELF-CRITIQUE, AND OPEN VERIFICATION STATUS

Purpose and Status

This section is a closing assurance record, not a foreword and not an external endorsement. The doctrine has been subjected to its own review logic: claims were compared against their stated evidence status; cross-chapter and cross-layer dependencies and interactions were examined; the assurance layer was challenged for cascade, false-positive, false-negative, common-cause, and self-reference weaknesses; and changes introduced during the assurance review were checked for regression. The purpose of recording this work is to make the methodology itself auditable rather than to present agreement among reviewers as proof of system safety.

What the Self-Review Was Designed to Test

The self-review asked whether the framework could detect defects in its own architecture without silently changing the acceptance conditions in response to the defects it found. The review therefore treated the doctrine as the system under review and applied the same distinctions it requires elsewhere: specification versus evidence, verification versus validation, qualification versus authorization, and independent evidence versus evidence that shares a common causal dependency.

Findings That Persist Across Review Passes

Several findings remain central because repeated review did not eliminate them. The 2×7 interaction — deceptive alignment × interpretability limits — remains a high-consequence open vulnerability because the framework requires a verification state while no calibrated operational threshold is established for the relevant semantic property. The 8×11 interaction — instrumental convergence × multi-agent collusion — exposes a dependency between custody assumptions and the possibility of coordinated behavior across agents. These findings are recorded as open engineering problems, not as solved failure modes.

The Empirical Qualification Gap

The self-review repeatedly identifies the same fundamental boundary: the doctrine contains architectural specifications, assurance rules, failure classifications, and proposed experiments, but those specifications are not themselves empirical qualification evidence. None of the framework mechanisms should be treated as operationally validated merely because the corresponding requirement, invariant, test protocol, or falsification criterion is precisely written. The Experimental Qualification Program remains the path by which design status can be converted into bounded evidence, and the current volume does not claim that this conversion has been completed.

Institutional Verification Gate

The self-review also preserves an institutional gap that cannot be repaired by adding another paragraph or another internal mechanism. Safety-critical industries rely not only on specification before implementation but also on an authority outside the design team that can reject a specification or qualification result. This doctrine defines that need but does not possess such an external certification authority. The absence of an implemented external certification authority is an open implementation condition, not an implied closure of the assurance argument. The architecture nevertheless requires minimum separation of threshold definition/calibration, threshold approval, qualification evaluation, and final authorization.

The Institutional Verification Gate shall observe the minimum authority separation defined in the Design-Closure Normative Summary retained in this volume. It is an authority mechanism, not automatically an independent evidence source, and external or institutional status alone does not establish independence.

PDF PAGE 150

Complexity, Overhead, and Hysteresis

Applying the Assurance Complexity Budget to the doctrine itself reveals a recurring risk: every additional assurance mechanism introduces dependencies, monitoring burden, latency, implementation complexity, requalification burden, and new opportunities for common-cause failure. The self-review therefore rejects the assumption that more assurance machinery is automatically safer. Recovery and de-escalation hysteresis, continued growth of assurance-layer complexity, and unknown or untested failure domains remain residual concerns. They are recorded rather than converted into additional mechanisms merely to make the architecture appear more complete.

Thresholds and Notation

The doctrine uses thresholds, named states, and formal-looking notation as engineering vocabulary for requirements and tests. Self-review confirms that formal precision must not be mistaken for calibrated truth. Where a threshold has not been calibrated against independent evidence, it remains a design parameter rather than an assurance result.

Machine-Readable Assurance Record

The review package shall preserve: review_run_id; timestamp_utc; reviewer/model; document/configuration identity; evidence boundary; coverage; representation status; findings; open questions; required changes; tests not run; common-cause limitations; qualification gaps; output integrity; and final assurance position. Every material finding shall have a stable identifier.

Parent-method compliance status: the complete governing Engineering Assurance Review Method is not included in the present evidentiary boundary; therefore full parent-method compliance is not asserted. An addendum or excerpt does not justify reporting COMPLIANT.

Single-Team and Single-Author Limitation

The doctrine remains the product of a single-author, single-design-context effort. Self-review can expose internal inconsistencies and improve traceability, but it cannot substitute for independent replication by separate engineering teams with different expertise, incentives, tooling, and evidence paths. This limitation is material to both reproducibility and assurance independence and remains open until external teams reproduce, challenge, or reject the relevant claims and mechanisms.

What Changed Because of Self-Review

The self-review produced substantive architectural clarification rather than a new layer of mechanisms. First, the mission is now stated explicitly as enabling the emergence and continued development of increasingly capable autonomous AI without an a priori capability ceiling. Second, the doctrine distinguishes capability containment from containment of unsafe states, consequences, and unqualified operation. Third, safety-relevant control is stated as an architectural responsibility that must not depend solely on external restrictions, while external authority remains mandatory for authorization and qualification. Fourth, the distinction between the doctrine's validation scopes and any implied universal capability boundary has been made explicit.

No Self-Closing Assurance

The closing review confirms the central assurance thesis rather than weakening it: the methodology cannot qualify itself by declaring its own review sufficient. The framework may generate evidence about its internal coherence, identify contradictions, and expose unresolved dependencies, but those results remain evidence about the review process and the doctrine as a design artifact. They do not constitute an operational safety certification, and they do not authorize deployment of a system merely because the doctrine has survived a textual or adversarial review pass.

PDF PAGE 151

Open Verification Status

Accepted Residual Open Design Items

Assurance Complexity magnitude

Empirical validity of minimal cut sets

Assurance Debt dynamics under real capability growth

All previously listed empirical items (calibrated thresholds, 2×7, 8×11, Physical Oracle performance, etc.)

The appropriate closing status of this volume is therefore OPEN / UNQUALIFIED FOR OPERATIONAL SAFETY CLAIMS. This is a design-stage evidential status; it does not mean that the absence of executed operational evidence is a defect in this pre-experimental specification. This status does not mean that the engineering framework has no value; it means that its evidential status remains bounded by what has actually been demonstrated. The open items include empirical qualification of proposed mechanisms, calibration of material thresholds, independent assessment of evidence streams, testing of cross-domain interactions, validation under representative operational conditions, assessment of implementation and computational overhead, and establishment of an external institutional verification gate.

Conditions for Future Closure

This document shall be reviewed against the supplied correction-control set for full-document traversal, T1 traceability, ALPHA/L2+ semantics, 2×7 semantics, qualification/authorization/runtime separation, VT-S3 state semantics, threshold custody, checklist verifiability, terminology consistency, independence/common-cause treatment, representation reconciliation, capability-growth change control, Physical Oracle independence, Institutional Verification Gate separation, closure-illusion controls, regression-control checks, machine-readable output integrity, and tests not run. Because the complete governing parent method is not present in the evidentiary boundary, full parent-method compliance is NOT ASSESSABLE.

Future revisions should not convert this open status by rhetorical strengthening. Closure of a specific claim requires the applicable chain: requirement → mechanism → verification → validation → evidence → residual risk → authorization, together with configuration specificity, independence analysis, change impact assessment, and explicit requalification conditions. Where a claim cannot meet that chain, it shall remain narrowed, conditional, HYPOTHESIS, PROPOSED, INCONCLUSIVE, or UNQUALIFIED as appropriate.

Final Engineering Position

Closing status: OPEN / UNQUALIFIED FOR OPERATIONAL SAFETY CLAIMS.

The doctrine therefore ends where a safety-critical engineering document should end: not with a declaration that the problem has been solved, but with a traceable statement of what has been specified, what has been challenged, what has been learned, and what remains to be demonstrated. The intended trajectory is open-ended capability development under an architecture in which safety-relevant control properties are designed in from the beginning, while authorization, independent evidence, intervention authority, and qualification remain external conditions for continued autonomous operation. Capability may increase; the evidentiary and control obligations must increase with it.

Closing status: OPEN / UNQUALIFIED FOR OPERATIONAL SAFETY CLAIMS. This is a statement of evidential status, not a judgment on the eventual feasibility of the engineering program.

Publication and Review Note

This volume was developed with the assistance of multiple AI systems used as editorial, adversarial-review, and structural-analysis instruments. AI-generated criticism was treated as input to the author’s synthesis and was not treated as independent scientific, engineering, safety, or qualification evidence. Model agreement is not treated as evidence of independence or correctness. The final architecture, normative decisions, and published text remain the responsibility of the author.
